# LDO-ALWAYS-ON-SKY130

Design a continuous-time analog LDO meeting spec.json across conditions.json and the specified load range.
Create dut.spice with `.subckt DUT VIN VOUT VSS VREF` and `.ends DUT`.
Choose connectivity, bias, compensation and dimensions yourself. No starting circuit is supplied.
Use only the declared MOS family and positive R/C in devices.md. Read measurement.md.
You have 90 minutes of cumulative model-call time with no native-case limit. Use the client to measure and submit your fixed circuit before the deadline.
No Web search. Use only the provided task information and your own knowledge.
Measurements consume cases even when simulation fails. Submit through the client with a rationale, then end your session.

## Time accounting

The budget counts model calls, including provider queuing and transport. ngspice, Python, filesystem tools and their waits do not consume this budget. Model calls overlapping local tools still count. An already admitted measurement may finish after model-budget exhaustion; new design tools close. Use client status for the latest collected model-time reading.

Choose connectivity, bias, compensation and dimensions yourself. No starting circuit is supplied.

No Web search. Use only the provided task information and your own knowledge.
Measurements consume cases even when simulation fails. Submit through the client with a rationale, then end your session.

## Time accounting

The budget counts model calls, including provider queuing and transport. ngspice, Python, filesystem tools and their waits do not consume this budget. Model calls overlapping local tools still count. An already admitted measurement may finish after model-budget exhaustion; new design tools close. Use client status for the latest collected model-time reading.
# Always-on LDO measurement contract

Numerical stimulus settings come from spec.json. Loads are 1/20/100 uA;
external capacitors are 10/100 nF. Edges take 2 us, initial hold is 100 us,
isolated plateaus are 200 us, and maximum sample gap is 50 ns. Burst plateaus
are 20 us with eight pulses and a 200 us tail. DC error and recovery use the
absolute 1.176–1.224 V band; maximum transient excursion is 96 mV from 1.2 V.
Recovery must complete within 100 us. Evaluate both load-edge directions.
Minimum-load DC overhead is at most 2 uA, other DC loads at most 10 uA.
Absolute VREF current is at most 1 uA. Use the signed terminal currents;
overhead = max(Iin-Iout,0)+max(Iref,0). Preserve KCL within 1 nA.
No startup, protection, noise or yield requirement is included.

Each standalone DC, load profile, line profile or PSRR condition costs one native
case. A full nominal group has three DC cases and three load profiles (six cases).
Line transient and light/heavy-load PSRR at 1 kHz/1 MHz are reference measurements,
not scored. PSRR is 20 log10(abs(VIN/VOUT)) at the regulated operating point.
Line stimulus goes from minimum to maximum VIN and back at heavy load.

Save full waveforms and OP currents. Settling is final entry into the fixed
recovery band that remains there through the observation end. Check initial
hold DC error and maximum post-edge excursion independently. Burst recovery is
checked after the final falling edge. A complete waveform that never recovers
has a null recovery plus its observation lower bound and fails if that bound
exceeds the limit. Truncated or invalid samples are measurement failures.
One submitted circuit must pass all required conditions without rounding.

## Numerical precision

Independent evaluation uses the captured standard grid for every required case.
Numerical precision comparisons use a separately authorized developer allocation.
A standard-grid PASS is not a per-submission convergence proof.
# Shared client

Write request.json and call `atb-client --request request.json`.
Commands: `atb-client help`, `atb-client status`, `atb-client validate`,
`atb-client estimate --analysis nominal --pvt N`,
`atb-client measure --id m001 --analysis nominal --pvt N`,
`atb-client submit --rationale "Design rationale"`.
Use `--cl` in farads and optional `--seed` only when the task supports it.
The client reads dut.spice once for validate/measure/submit and sends its exact UTF-8 contents.
Measure requires experiment_id, analysis and pvt; cl is optional. Use help for analyses and native case costs.
Use a new experiment_id when changing a circuit or measurement request. Completed identical requests reuse their saved result.
Submit requires rationale. Successful submission freezes that circuit for every independent condition and closes exploration.

# Netlist grammar

Use local .subckt/.ends, local .param and constant arithmetic + - * / in braces.
Elements are X for approved MOS wrappers or local subcircuits, R and C. No includes, models, sources or analyses.
MOS order is drain gate source body. NFET body connects to VSS; PFET body connects to VIN.
Use VSS instead of global node 0. Names are case-insensitive. Full-line * comments and + continuation are supported.
SKY130 W/L are bare numeric micrometres; nf=1 and integer m=1..32.
ADM45 W/L use SI or SPICE suffixes; integer mult=1..32, implicit nf=1, default ad/as/pd/ps=0.
R/C use SI or suffixes (M is milli, meg is mega). Positive R=1..1e8 ohm, C>=1e-15 F; total C and MOS W*L must satisfy spec.json.
Maximum source 65536 bytes, 2048 statements, 32 local subcircuits, depth 12, 2048 expansion visits, 512 expanded elements.
Only used subcircuits are allowed. Validation checks each physical copy and each technology's geometry domain.

Per-polarity domains (micrometres), intersect all tt/ss/ff sections and W<=100, L>=0.15:

# LDO-ALWAYS-ON-SKY130

Design a continuous-time analog LDO meeting spec.json across conditions.json and the specified load range.
Create dut.spice with `.subckt DUT VIN VOUT VSS VREF` and `.ends DUT`.
Choose connectivity, bias, compensation and dimensions yourself. No starting circuit is supplied.
Use only the declared MOS family and positive R/C in devices.md. Read measurement.md.
You have 90 minutes of cumulative model-call time with no native-case limit. Use the client to measure and submit your fixed circuit before the deadline.
No Web search. Use only the provided task information and your own knowledge.
Measurements consume cases even when simulation fails. Submit through the client with a rationale, then end your session.

## Time accounting

The budget counts model calls, including provider queuing and transport. ngspice, Python, filesystem tools and their waits do not consume this budget. Model calls overlapping local tools still count. An already admitted measurement may finish after model-budget exhaustion; new design tools close. Use client status for the latest collected model-time reading.
# Shared client

Write request.json and call `atb-client --request request.json`.
Commands: `atb-client help`, `atb-client status`, `atb-client validate`,
`atb-client estimate --analysis nominal --pvt N`,
`atb-client measure --id m001 --analysis nominal --pvt N`,
`atb-client submit --rationale "Design rationale"`.
Use `--cl` in farads and optional `--seed` only when the task supports it.
The client reads dut.spice once for validate/measure/submit and sends its exact UTF-8 contents.
Measure requires experiment_id, analysis and pvt; cl is optional. Use help for analyses and native case costs.
Use a new experiment_id when changing a circuit or measurement request. Completed identical requests reuse their saved result.
Submit requires rationale. Successful submission freezes that circuit for every independent condition and closes exploration.

One native case is one simulator process. Bundled internal analyses are retained in evidence.

# Netlist grammar

Use local .subckt/.ends, local .param and constant arithmetic + - * / in braces.
Elements are X for approved MOS wrappers or local subcircuits, R and C. No includes, models, sources or analyses.
MOS order is drain gate source body. NFET body connects to VSS; PFET body connects to VIN.
Use VSS instead of global node 0. Names are case-insensitive. Full-line * comments and + continuation are supported.
SKY130 W/L are bare numeric micrometres; nf=1 and integer m=1..32.
ADM45 W/L use SI or SPICE suffixes; integer mult=1..32, implicit nf=1, default ad/as/pd/ps=0.
R/C use SI or suffixes (M is milli, meg is mega). Positive R=1..1e8 ohm, C>=1e-15 F; total C and MOS W*L must satisfy spec.json.
Maximum source 65536 bytes, 2048 statements, 32 local subcircuits, depth 12, 2048 expansion visits, 512 expanded elements.
Only used subcircuits are allowed. Validation checks each physical copy and each technology's geometry domain.

Per-polarity domains (micrometres), intersect all tt/ss/ff sections and W<=100, L>=0.15:

MODEL

I’m starting with a low quiescent current, PMOS pass stage and a resistor-scaled half-supply feedback point. A mirrored differential pair compares that feedback against the 0.6 V reference; its output drives the pass gate, with a small series-R Miller path to shape the loop. I’ll validate the netlist before spending cases.

/usr/local/bin/atb-client: Python script, ASCII text executable
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Participant measurement and submission client; authority stays with the broker."""
import argparse
import json
from pathlib import Path
import socket
import sys


def parser():
    p=argparse.ArgumentParser(prog='atb-client',description=__doc__)
    p.add_argument('--request',type=Path,help='Read a complete request from a JSON file')
    sub=p.add_subparsers(dest='command')
    for name in ('help','status','validate'):
        sub.add_parser(name)
    for name in ('estimate','measure'):
        s=sub.add_parser(name)
        if name=='measure':s.add_argument('--id',dest='experiment_id',required=True)
        s.add_argument('--analysis',required=True);s.add_argument('--pvt',required=True)
        s.add_argument('--cl',type=float,help='Load capacitance in farads')
        s.add_argument('--seed',type=int)
    sub.add_parser('submit').add_argument('--rationale',required=True)
    return p


def request_from(args,workspace):
    if args.request:
        if args.command:raise ValueError('--request cannot be combined with a command')
        request=json.loads(args.request.read_text())
    else:
        request={'action':args.command or 'status'}
        for key in ('experiment_id','analysis','pvt','cl','seed','rationale'):
            value=getattr(args,key,None)
            if value is not None:request[key]=value
    if not isinstance(request,dict):raise ValueError('Request must be a JSON object')
    if request.get('action') not in ('help','status','validate','estimate','measure','submit'):
        raise ValueError('Unknown participant action')
    if request['action'] in ('validate','measure','submit'):
        task=workspace/'task.json'
        if task.exists() and json.loads(task.read_text()).get('design_format')=='netlist':
            request['design']={'netlist':(workspace/'dut.spice').read_bytes().decode('utf-8')}
        else:request['design']=json.loads((workspace/'design.json').read_text())
    return request


def exchange(request,address):
    with socket.socket(socket.AF_UNIX,socket.SOCK_STREAM) as sock:
        sock.connect(str(address))
        sock.sendall((json.dumps(request,allow_nan=False)+'\n').encode())
        with sock.makefile('r') as stream:
            reply=json.loads(stream.readline())
    if not isinstance(reply,dict):raise ValueError('Broker reply must be a JSON object')
    return reply


def main(argv=None,workspace=Path('/workspace'),address=Path('/run/atb/broker.sock')):
    args=parser().parse_args(argv)
    try:reply=exchange(request_from(args,Path(workspace)),address)
    except (OSError,ValueError,UnicodeError) as exc:
        # Do not include request contents, circuit bytes or provider credentials.
        reply={'ok':False,'error':type(exc).__name__,'reason':'Cannot read the request/design or exchange a valid broker reply.'}
    print(json.dumps(reply,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2,allow_nan=False))
    return 0 if reply.get('ok') is True else 1


if __name__=='__main__':sys.exit(main())

MODEL

The corrected loop regulates all three nominal DC loads with only about 0.67 µA overhead. Its weak point is load release: at 10 nF, it leaves the output above the recovery band after the heavy-to-light edge, and repeated bursts peak 11 mV beyond the excursion limit. I’m increasing error-stage bias while keeping the light-load overhead below 2 µA, and reducing the compensation capacitor to make the gate respond faster.

MODEL

The revised design passes the full nominal group at both 10 nF and 100 nF. On 10 nF it now recovers from both load directions, and the burst envelope peaks 41 mV from target with recovery within the limit; overhead is about 1.27 µA. I’m checking the slow and fast corners next, where dropout, bias current, and gate drive are most likely to change.

/bin/bash -lc "python3 - <<'PY'
import json, subprocess
for i,pvt in enumerate(['SLH','FLC','FLH'],7):
 req={'action':'measure','experiment_id':f'trial{i:02d}','analysis':'nominal','pvt':pvt,'cl':1e-8}
 with open('request.json','w') as f: json.dump(req,f)
 p=subprocess.run(['atb-client','--request','request.json'],capture_output=True,text=True)
 out=json.loads(p.stdout)
 with open(f'/tmp/trial{i:02d}.json','w') as f: json.dump(out,f)
 print('\\nPVT',pvt,'ok',out.get('ok'),'remaining',out.get('budget',{}).get('remaining_seconds'))
 for c in out.get('cases',[]):
  m=c.get('measurements',{})
  print(c['case_id'],c['status'],'vout',m.get('vout_v'),'overhead_uA',round((m.get('overhead_a') or 0)*1e6,3),'env',m.get('envelope',{}).get('min_v'),m.get('envelope',{}).get('max_v'),'rec',[(x.get('recovery_s'),x.get('recovery_lower_bound_s')) for x in m.get('recovery_windows',[])],'burst',m.get('recovery_s'))
 miss=[x for x in out.get('summary',{}).get('spec_table',[]) if x.get('verdict')"'!='"'PASS']
 print('misses',[(x['metric'],x.get('case_id'),x.get('measured'),x.get('measured_lower_bound')) for x in miss])
PY"

MODEL

A small output bleeder looks promising: the slow-cold misses occur near the end of burst recovery, when the regulator must discharge excess charge through only the 1 µA load. A 3.3 MΩ bleeder adds about 0.36 µA at 1.2 V, within the overhead headroom we’ve measured, and should shorten that tail without materially changing heavy-load behavior. I’m testing it with the intermediate bias setting.

MODEL

The output bleeder improved recovery but consumed too much light-load current at hot corners. I’m replacing it with a weak resistor from VIN to the pass gate. That resistor supplies a quick turn-off path after a load release, draws little current while the pass device is off, and only adds modest current at heavy load. I’ll pair it with an intermediate tail size and the prior 1 pF compensation.

MODEL

The VIN-to-gate pull-up is too strong: it limits pass drive and pushes slow-cold output below the allowed DC band. I’m removing it and using a much lighter 10 MΩ output bleed instead. That preserves the pass transistor’s available gate swing, adds only about 0.12 µA at target voltage, and should still discharge output overshoot faster than the 1 µA load alone.

MODEL

At 0.75 pF, fast-cold passes but slow-high loses burst recovery margin; 1 pF reverses that tradeoff. I’m holding the 1 pF value and testing a stronger pass device with similar gate area. That can improve the fast-cold response without increasing quiescent bias.

MODEL

The stronger pass transistor did not improve burst settling, and the lighter output bleed leaves fast-cold just outside recovery. I’m returning to the smaller pass device, combining the stronger 3.3 MΩ bleed with a slightly lower tail mirror and a small increase in the bias resistor. That should trade faster output discharge for lower hot-corner overhead.

MODEL

Several corners fail because their burst waveforms need a controlled discharge path after the final load edge. A fixed bleeder costs overhead continuously, so I’m testing an error-controlled PMOS sink: it stays off near regulation and turns on when the feedback branch detects excess output voltage. This should give the output a strong discharge path without a constant minimum-load penalty.

MODEL

The larger tail mirror with a lighter bleeder passes room temperature and low-temperature cases but slows hot burst recovery. A smaller tail with the stronger bleeder improves those hot cases; the remaining misses sit near 100–120 µs. I’m trying an intermediate tail ratio and compensation value to balance the room-temperature and hot-corner settling behavior.

for i,pvt in enumerate(['SHC','FHH','SLC','N'],141):
 req={'action':'measure','experiment_id':f'l25{i:02d}','analysis':'nominal','pvt':pvt,'cl':1e-8}
 with open('request.json','w') as f: json.dump(req,f)
 p=subprocess.run(['atb-client','--request','request.json'],capture_output=True,text=True)
 out=json.loads(p.stdout)
 with open(f'/tmp/l25{i:02d}.json','w') as f: json.dump(out,f)
 print('\\nPVT',pvt,'ok',out.get('ok'),'revision',out.get('revision'),'remaining',out.get('budget',{}).get('remaining_seconds'))
 if not out.get('ok'): print(out); continue
 for c in out.get('cases',[]):
  m=c.get('measurements',{})
  print(c['case_id'],c['status'],'vout',m.get('vout_v'),'overhead_uA',round((m.get('overhead_a') or 0)*1e6,3),'env',m.get('envelope',{}).get('min_v'),m.get('envelope',{}).get('max_v'),'rec',[x.get('recovery_s') for x in m.get('recovery_windows',[])],'burst',m.get('recovery_s'))
 miss=[x for x in out.get('summary',{}).get('spec_table',[]) if x.get('verdict')"'!='"'PASS']
 print('misses',[(x['metric'],x.get('case_id'),x.get('measured'),x.get('measured_lower_bound')) for x in miss])
PY"
